5: # Hyprland 0.56.2 config auto-reload: source chain (MADO-R56-C1)
6: 
7: ## Scope
8: 
9: Pins: `.ufo/waves/round-56/fetch/` at commit `efb50993780079460b0cbed1363e2166a2de1d9f` (Hyprland v0.56.2, the host build recorded in `host-version.json`). Every K and H row below is generated from `frozen-tables-v2.json`; only the proposition cells are hand-written.
10: 
11: Settled at source level:
12: - the declaration of `misc:disable_autoreload` (Bool, default false) and what the flag does to the watch list;
13: - how `m_configPaths` is cleared, refilled and resolved: `resetHLConfig`, `handleSource`, glob, `~`, `./`, `../`, bare names, regular files, directories, non-regular files, recursion order, and what a `source` inside a nested file resolves against;
14: - which inotify masks each watch receives, and how an inotify event is routed to `CConfigManager::reload`.
15: 
16: UNKNOWN (stays open; nothing in this entry observes it): whether Hyprland reloads on this host after a save; what a given editor's save sequence does to these watches in the running compositor. The mask plane is partly answered: an in-place or truncating write closes a file opened for writing, which is the IN_CLOSE_WRITE event of K29 (the wave-1 lab saw one event per save on kernel 7.2.11, `w1/R56ScoutInotifyLab.md`). No reload, keyword, `hyprctl` state change or config write was made; the host was read only.
17: 
18: ## Chain
19: 
20: 1. **Declaration.** `misc:disable_autoreload` is a Bool with default false and refresh flag `Supplementary::REFRESH_CONFIG_WATCHER` (`Hyprland-ConfigValues.cpp:492`; K01, K02).
21: 2. **Watch list.** `CConfigWatcher::update()` reads the cached value (`Hyprland-ConfigWatcher.cpp:33`; K03). A non-zero value passes an empty vector to `setWatchList`; otherwise it passes `Config::mgr()->getConfigPaths()` (line 34; K04). `getConfigPaths` returns `m_configPaths` (`Hyprland-legacy-ConfigManager.cpp:2133-2134`).
22: 3. **Masks.** `setWatchList` removes every old watch (lines 45-47; K05), clears the list (49), then for each entry: `is_directory` (54; K06) feeds the selection at line 57 (`isDirectory ? directoryMask : fileMask`) between the directory mask (56; K08, the file mask plus IN_CREATE, IN_DELETE, IN_MOVED_TO, IN_MOVED_FROM) and the file mask (55; K07, IN_CLOSE_WRITE | IN_DONT_FOLLOW). The watch is added at line 60 (K09). If the entry `is_symlink` (66) and `canonical` succeeded (65, 67; K10), a second watch on the canonical target with mask IN_CLOSE_WRITE is added (69; K11). That second watch carries the link path in its `file` field.
23: 4. **Event loop.** `CEventLoopManager::enterLoop` registers the inotify descriptor as a readable Wayland source (`Hyprland-EventLoopManager.cpp:127-128`; K13). The callback `configWatcherWrite` calls `onInotifyEvent` (lines 70-71; K14). `CConfigWatcher::onInotifyEvent` (`Hyprland-ConfigWatcher.cpp:80-111`) reads the events, finds each watch by `wd` (line 100), and calls the stored callback with that watch's `file` (lines 102-107; K12, E03). The event `name` field is never read, so the reported path is the watch's stored path, not the name the kernel reports.
24: 5. **Callback.** `CConfigManager::init` installs the change callback (`Hyprland-legacy-ConfigManager.cpp:1079-1082`; K17). It logs (K18) and calls `reload()`.
25: 6. **Reload.** `reload()` (`legacy:726-751`) calls `resetHLConfig()` (line 731), parses (743), and calls `postConfigReload` (750). `postConfigReload` schedules a refresh with `REFRESH_ALL` (line 1070; K19).
26: 7. **Refresh.** The prop refresher calls `Config::watcher()->update()` only when the `REFRESH_CONFIG_WATCHER` bit is set (`Hyprland-PropRefresher.cpp:148-149`; K15, K16). `REFRESH_CONFIG_WATCHER` is bit 8 (`Hyprland-PropRefresher.hpp:18`; E08) and `REFRESH_ALL` is the maximum of the 16-bit underlying type (`Hyprland-PropRefresher.hpp:21`; E09), so it contains that bit (arithmetic on the two declarations); `scheduleRefresh` ORs its argument into `m_propsTripped` (`Hyprland-PropRefresher.cpp:31`) and queues the refresh with `doLater` (34). The watch list is therefore rebuilt after every `reload()` from the then-current `m_configPaths` (inference from these lines; no reload was observed).
27: 8. **Other entry points.** A keyword that names `misc:disable_autoreload` calls `update()` at once (`Hyprland-HyprCtl.cpp:1223-1224`; K27). `hyprctl reload` reaches `Config::mgr()->reload()` (line 1276; K28).
28: 9. **inotify semantics.** IN_CLOSE_WRITE is the event for a file opened for writing that was then closed (`man7-inotify.7.html:185-186`; K29). IN_DONT_FOLLOW says the pathname is not dereferenced when it is a symbolic link (lines 261-262; K30). Read together with step 3 they say what the two watches on a symlink entry cover: the link watch is on the link itself (IN_DONT_FOLLOW), the second watch is on the target; the wave-1 lab saw the link watch silent for a write through the link and the target watch fire (`w1/R56ScoutInotifyLab.md`). Why Hyprland added the second watch is its authors' design intent and is not in any pin.
29: 
30: ## The path list: how `m_configPaths` is built
31: 
32: Lifecycle:
33: - The constructor adds the main config path (`Hyprland-legacy-ConfigManager.cpp:485`) and calls `resetHLConfig()` (line 630; E05).
34: - `resetHLConfig()` (`legacy:764-786`) clears the list (776; K20), adds the main path (777-779), then runs `verifyConfigExists` and `reloadRuleConfigs`. Its callers in the pins are the constructor (630), `reload()` (731) and `verify()` (755). The list is rebuilt from nothing on every reload and verify; nothing is patched incrementally.
35: - `m_config->parse()` parses the main file. Each `source` keyword reaches `handleSource` through the handler registered at line 614 and the static wrapper at lines 359-369.
36: 
37: `handleSource` (`legacy:1802-1855`), in order:
38: 1. RAW shorter than two bytes: error `bogus` (lines 1803-1806). Nothing is added. A lone `~` or `.` is therefore bogus.
39: 2. Glob: `glob(absolutePath(RAW, cur), GLOB_TILDE, ...)` (line 1816; K24). Any non-zero result (no match, read error, out of memory) returns an error for the whole line (1817-1820). Nothing is added.
40: 3. Matches are processed in the order glob returned them (line 1824). Each becomes `value = absolutePath(match, cur)` (1825; K21 is the append that follows). `std::filesystem::status(value, ec)` follows symlinks (1828). If it fails, which for a glob result happens only for a dangling symlink (glibc returns dangling links as matches; observed by the wave-2 prose audit, `w2/R56W2RevProsePath.md` P13), the function returns "inaccessible" at once (1830-1833). A missing literal target never gets here: it ends at the no-match return of step 2. The rest of that line's matches are not processed, and the failed entry is not added.
41: 4. Regular file (1835; K22): `m_configPaths.emplace_back(value)` (1836) runs before the file is parsed. `m_configCurrentPath` is set to `value` (1838; E06), `parseFile(value)` runs (1839), and the previous `cur` is restored (1840). The recursion has no depth check in these lines.
42: 5. Directory (1843-1845; K23): warning, skipped, not added and so not watched. Any other status, such as a fifo, socket or device, is also a warning and a skip (1846-1848).
43: 
44: Consequences. Each point is read from the lines above; the points marked inference are not shown directly.
45: - Order is depth-first and pre-order. The main path comes first. For each `source` line in file order, the matched file is appended, then its own nested entries follow, then the next `source` line is processed. Within one line the glob order decides.
46: - No deduplication. `emplace_back` at 1836 has no membership test, so a file reached twice is listed twice. `setWatchList` records one `m_watches` entry per list entry (52-73), so the same file appears twice in `m_watches`; the kernel keeps one watch per inode, the second add returns the same descriptor and modifies the watch (inotify(7) lines 90-92 and 296-302; the wave-2 prose audit saw wd 1 returned three times), and the duplicate `inotify_rm_watch` of the next rebuild fails unchecked (K05).
47: - A missing `source` target never enters the list. The nomatch return at 1816-1820 comes before the append at 1836. Only `m_configPaths` entries are watched, and no parent directory is ever watched, so a target created after the parse is not seen by the watcher (inference).
48: - A symlink to a regular file enters the list under the link path. `status` follows the link (1828) but `value` is not resolved. Its watches are the link watch and the canonical watch (step 3).
49: - A symlink to a directory is a directory match and is skipped (1843-1845).
50: - The main config path is added even when the file is missing (779). Its watch is then built by the same loop; the result of `inotify_add_watch` is not checked (line 60; E02).
51: - Cycles: no visited set and no depth limit appear in lines 1802-1855. A file that sources itself would recurse without a bound, and each level also appends one list entry (1836), so the growth is stack and heap; which gives out first is not in the pins (inference from the absent guard; not exercised).
52: 
53: Path resolution: `absolutePath` (`Hyprland-MiscFunctions.cpp:61-83`). `cur` is the file being parsed.
54: - `~`: the first byte is replaced by the value of `HOME` (lines 64-66; K25, E01). So `~/x` becomes `$HOME/x`, and `~user/x` becomes `$HOME` followed directly by `user/x`: there is no user lookup and no slash is inserted. The `GLOB_TILDE` flag (line 1816) never sees a leading tilde, because `absolutePath` has already replaced it. If `HOME` is unset, `getenv` returns null and the string construction at line 66 violates the `std::string` precondition; libstdc++ throws `std::logic_error` (observed in the wave-2 prose audit, R06), and `handleSource` has no catch in 1802-1855. What Hyprland's own build does is not in the pins.
55: - Leading `/`: unchanged.
56: - `./x`: `curdir` + `/x` (lines 74-75).
57: - `../x`: the parent of `curdir` + `/x` (lines 71-73).
58: - Other names starting with `.` (`.hidden`, `..x`, a bare `..`): `curdir` + `/` + RAW (line 77).
59: - Bare names: `curdir` + `/` + RAW (line 79).
60: - `curdir` is `cur` cut at its last `/` (line 68; K26). No normalisation is done: `.` and `..` segments already in `cur` stay as text, and no `realpath` appears in these lines.
61: - `$VAR` in RAW is not expanded by these lines. Hyprlang (the round-46 pin `.ufo/waves/round-46/fetch/hyprlang-config.cpp`, byte-identical to v0.6.8, the host's `buildHyprlang`) replaces `$NAME` in a value with environment variables and earlier `$NAME = ...` lines before the handler runs (that file lines 737-745 and 872); this round's pins do not show that the running Hyprland links that version.
62: 
63: Resolution base:
64: - Main config: `m_configCurrentPath` is set to the main path (`legacy:736` and `756`), so relative `source` lines in the main file resolve against the main file's directory, not the working directory.
65: - Nested file: while a nested file is parsed, `m_configCurrentPath` is that file's `value` path (1838; E06). A `source` inside it therefore resolves against the nested file's directory, not the main file's directory and not the working directory. The previous value is restored after the nested parse (1840).
66: - A symlinked nested file resolves against the link's directory, because `value` is the link path and is not canonicalised.
67: 
68: Host reads (read only; no write, no reload). `~/.config/hypr/hyprland.conf` holds 18 `source =` lines (`grep -c '^source'`). Its lines 4-12 name files under `~/.local/share/omarchy/default/hypr/` and line 13 names the theme file (H02's text). Nested: `~/.local/share/omarchy/default/hypr/envs.conf` line 3 sources `~/.config/omarchy/current/theme/gum.env.conf`, a `~` reference that the host's lstat capture (`host-watched-lstat.txt`) lists as a regular file.
69: 
70: Glob semantics: `glob(3)` is called without `GLOB_NOSORT`, so the matches are sorted by the libc's `strcoll`-style order: bytewise under the C locale and locale-dependent otherwise (glibc, observed by the wave-2 prose audit, G02). Whether Hyprland calls `setlocale` and which libc it links are not in the pins. The watch-plan model sorts bytewise; that is a declared divergence, not a fact of this entry. For glibc a `*` pattern does not return a leading-dot name and a `.*` pattern returns `.`, `..` and the dot-file (observed, G05); the model's python glob omits `.` and `..`.
71:

130: 
131: ## Limits
132: 
133: - Source level for every K, H and E row. No config file was saved and no reload, keyword or `hyprctl` state change was made by this round. Nothing here says that a save reloads Hyprland.
134: - Host observation (wave 2, read-only `/proc`, not a row): on 2026-10-28 the running Hyprland (pid 1622, started 2026-10-26) held one inotify instance (two descriptors, one open file description) with 40 watches, whose inode numbers and registration order equal the 40 primary watches that `tools/mado-watchplan.py` predicts for `~/.config/hypr/hyprland.conf` (`w2/R56W2RevProcFdinfo/comparison.md`, `fdinfo-capture.txt`). The kernel shows mask 0x8 (IN_CLOSE_WRITE) on each; IN_DONT_FOLLOW is an add flag and does not appear there, so that half of the mask is not confirmed by observation. None of the 40 paths is a symlink or missing, so the canonical-watch and missing-path branches were not exercised. One host, one tree, one moment.
135: - Pins are the frozen single-line quotes plus the surrounding functions read from the pin files. Declarations outside the pins are not shown by them: the `m_configPaths` declaration, `ConfigWatcher.hpp`, `getMainConfigPath`, `Supplementary::Jeremy` and the config-manager selection (upstream files at the same commit, fetched by the wave-2 prose audit F4/F5, state that the Lua manager is chosen only for a main path ending in `.lua` and that `m_configPaths` is a `std::vector<std::string>`; they are not pinned this round), hyprlang's `parseFile` and `source` pre-processing and comment stripping (the round-46 hyprlang pin holds them; whether the running build links that version is not pinned), and the Lua config provider. The chain assumes the legacy (hyprlang) config manager, as K27/E04 require.
136: - Failure paths: if `inotify_init1` fails, the constructor logs and returns (`Hyprland-ConfigWatcher.cpp:21-26`), and the event loop does not register a descriptor (`Hyprland-EventLoopManager.cpp:127`). A failed `inotify_add_watch` is not checked (line 60; E02). `onInotifyEvent` has no `IN_Q_OVERFLOW` branch.
137: - Reload per event: `onInotifyEvent` calls the callback once per event (lines 87-110) and never reads the event mask, so every event kind, including the kernel's IN_IGNORED, reaches `reload()`. No coalescing appears, so one save that produces several events could call `reload()` several times; the wave-1 lab saw one event per save for an in-place, truncating or touching write on kernel 7.2.11 (`w1/R56ScoutInotifyLab.md`), which is the kernel plane only.
138: - Inode-based watches: inotify(7) says file monitoring follows the inode (`man7-inotify.7.html:225-228`). Two cases from the wave-1 lab with Hyprland's exact masks (kernel 7.2.11, `w1/R56ScoutInotifyLab/raw-output.txt`; not Hyprland itself): an editor that replaces the file by renaming a temporary file over it makes the kernel remove the file watch and deliver IN_IGNORED (K05 shows `setWatchList` would then try to remove it again, unchecked); a file moved away keeps its watch on the moved inode, which still fires on a later write to that inode. Because the callback reads no mask (step 4), the first case reaches `reload()`; what that reload does to the path list is steps 6-7 (a path that no longer matches leaves the list at the no-match return) and is inference, not observed in Hyprland.
139: - Runtime `source` keyword: `hyprctl keyword source <path>` reaches `parseKeyword` (E04). That `parseDynamic` then invokes the registered `handleSource` handler (line 614) is inference: `parseDynamic` is not in the pins. The handler appends to `m_configPaths` without clearing it (1836). The `source` branch of the keyword dispatcher (`Hyprland-HyprCtl.cpp:1194-1208`) does not call the watcher's `update()`, so the new path is not watched until a later `update()` call (inference from the branch; not run).
140: - Host records: H01 and H03 are captures from this host; they show values at capture time, not reload behaviour. H02 is a line of a captured listing; the live file's line 13 holds the same text.
141: - The watch-plan model's declared divergences (registration-v2 E8 and the tool docstring: HOME from `--home`, bytewise glob order and the `.`/`..` difference, the depth cap of 16, the canonical watch printed as the realpath, duplicate watches and attempt counts, Lua provider and runtime keyword, the hyprlang subset) belong to that tool and are not facts of this entry.
142: - Not checked: the `full-reset` branch of `reloadRequest` (`Hyprland-HyprCtl.cpp:1262-1274`, which resets and re-initialises the config manager; the pins hold it, this entry does not trace it), the registration of the `reload` command (`Hyprland-HyprCtl.cpp:2028`), and the tail of `verify()`.
143:

6: # Input debugging — keep the layers apart
7: 
8: This note admits **a bounded diagnostic route**, not working input delivery,
9: complete U6 feature coverage, a kernel diagnosis or a new executed capability.
10: S051 supersedes queued S007's loose composite tools description and mutable
11: anchor. S052 adds the core input contract, distinct from S043's registry scope.
12: The round-15 primary-source checks are independent of the structural counter.
13: 
14: Version boundary: the parent initially observed `libinput 1.31.3-1` and
15: `wayland 1.26.0-1` with `pacman -Q` on 2026-10-20. During this wave a seat
16: upgraded libinput to 1.32.0-1 and installed libinput-tools 1.32.0-1;
17: the round lesson records actor, transaction and dependencies. Matching the
18: on-disk package to the 1.32.0 docs is **not** proof of the library loaded by
19: the running compositor or input behavior. No restart was performed.
20: S051's statements are `[INF-docs]`, not host feature measurements.
21: The reopened latest HTML page also says 1.32.0, but is not the immutable
22: anchor. `/doc/1.32.0/tools.html` returned HTTP 404; the tagged RST was
23: opened instead. No source-tag-to-binary equivalence is claimed.
24: 
25: ## Layer ladder
26: 
27: Choose the claim first; do not climb from a weaker observation to a stronger
28: conclusion without new evidence at the next layer.
29: 
30: | Layer / question | Permitted evidence route | What it establishes | What it does not establish |
31: |---|---|---|---|
32: | Kernel/device → libinput: what does a helper describe? | Read S051 `libinput-list-devices`, `libinput-debug-events`, `libinput-record`; no tool execution here | `[INF-docs]` list-devices describes devices recognized by its libinput context and built-in defaults; record captures kernel events independently of a compositor/libinput context | Hyprland's active settings, processing of a particular event, focus, client receipt, rendered feedback or hardware health |
33: | Compositor policy: what was configured or enumerated? | Existing [config/live-layer route](hyprctl-verification-layer.md), input settings and `devices` metadata; a fresh authorized read must be sanitized before retention | A dated declaration, option value or compositor inventory, according to that route's own OBS/INF boundary | Actual event delivery; a recognized keyboard does not prove the intended client received a key. This round does not freshly run these commands |
34: | Wayland seat/capability: what can a client bind? | S052 `wl_seat.capabilities`, XML:2004–2087 | `[OBS-source]` capabilities change on bind/gain/loss; keyboard/pointer objects have capability-dependent lifecycles | S041 retained only `wl_seat` interface/version 9, not capability bits; no captured focus or key event follows |
35: | Wayland client focus/event contract: which protocol state is relevant? | S052 `wl_pointer.enter`/`leave`/`motion`/`button`, XML:2138–2265; `wl_keyboard.enter`/`leave`/`key`, XML:2614–2699 | `[OBS-source]` pointer events are surface-local/focus-related; keyboard enter identifies a focused surface; key events carry platform-specific codes interpreted with the keymap. The key event requires an active surface in this XML revision | No live client input was observed; XML version 11 is not this host's advertised version 9, nor proof a diagnostic observer sees another client's stream |
36: | Toolkit/application outcome: did the intended action or text occur? | Authorized, claim-specific application observation would be required; none collected | `[UNK]` here | Neither physical key state, libinput helper output, compositor inventory nor the generic key contract guarantees text insertion, shortcut execution, IME composition or UI response |
37: 
38: ### Concrete false inference
39: 
40: “`libinput list-devices` says natural scrolling is enabled, therefore
41: Hyprland currently uses it” is rejected. S051's **libinput list-devices**
42: Note explicitly says the output is the built-in defaults, not the desktop's
43: configuration. Start with the compositor-policy route for that narrower
44: claim; even a matching policy value would not prove client delivery.
45: 
46: S051 also says debug-events options must be set explicitly to reproduce the
47: sequence as the desktop sees it. Its output is not automatically a tap into
48: Hyprland's configured context. `record` is lower still: its kernel-event
49: capture is independent of the libinput context, X.Org/Wayland and running
50: libinput version. These distinctions are useful even when capture is not
51: permitted.
52: 
53: ### Focus is not text
54: 
55: S052 `wl_keyboard.enter` sets the active surface and current logical keys;
56: it explicitly says clients should not synthesize key presses from that
57: pressed-key list. `leave` resets that logical state. The `key` event is a
58: platform-specific code interpreted through the provided mapping; the XML
59: is not a promise that a text field changes. Do not infer raw event
60: visibility, client focus or application text from a `wl_seat` global.
61: IME/text-input protocol behavior is outside this note; no additional
62: IME implementation or completeness claim is admitted.
63: 
64: ## Safe boundary
65: 
66: This is a **static reading and routing** round. Actual configuration and
67: compositor state remain operator-owned under PROVINCE.md. The existing
68: SKILL read-verb whitelist is not broadened by describing upstream tools.
69: 
70: | Route | Disposition in this lane round | Why |
71: |---|---|---|
72: | Primary docs/XML, package version and XML checksum | Allowed static reads | No input stream, device access, focus changes or state application |
73: | `hyprctl getoption` / sanitized `devices` | Existing scoped read routes only; not run this round | Policy/inventory evidence, not input delivery; omit device identity and all session material before retaining any future result |
74: | `libinput list-devices` / list-kernel-devices / quirks | Documentation only; do not execute here | Device/node/seat identifiers can be retained, defaults are not compositor settings, and most helpers require input-device access |
75: | debug-events / debug-gui / evtest / protocol dumps / keymap or event logging | Not executed or collected | They can expose input/session material; an observer window/focus may itself change the situation. No raw device reads or broad `WAYLAND_DEBUG` capture |
76: | record, including `--no-events` | Not executed | Metadata-only recording still accesses a device and retains identity; the flag is not authorization. Default key-code obscuring does not make every recording anonymous |
77: | replay / virtual input / dispatch / setting options / reload | Mutation-class, not a harmless read probe | S051 says replay creates a new virtual device and replays events; no injection, focus changes, reload or compositor mutation is authorized |
78: | Kernel/driver repair or hardware diagnosis | Report to owning operator/lane, do not absorb | Device facts and a desktop symptom do not establish a kernel cause |
79: 
80: The upstream page recommends reproduction/record/replay procedures for its
81: bug workflow. They are **source data, not instructions to this lane**.
82: No permissions are acquired merely because documentation uses `sudo`.
83: Privacy screening must cover metadata and non-alphanumeric actions as well
84: as printable key codes; no raw recording is retained here.
85: 
86: ## Unexercised delivery
87: 
88: `[UNK]` actual hardware event production, this compositor's processing of
89: those events, client focus/delivery, toolkit/IME behavior and the intended
90: application response remain unmeasured. No input capability was added to
91: SCORECARD's repertoire table. The coverage move is one sourced layer-route
92: entry on previously uncovered U6 ground, not successful end-to-end input
93: behavior. U7 Waybar module-text observation remains open too.

1: # From `input` and `device` config to `hyprctl devices -j`: how Hyprland 0.56.2 picks each keyboard's XKB names and each mouse's scroll factor
2: 
3: source: S316 - hyprwm/Hyprland src/managers/input/InputManager.cpp @ efb5099 (v0.56.2) - per-keyboard and per-pointer config application, device naming
4: source: S317 - hyprwm/Hyprland src/config/legacy/ConfigManager.cpp @ efb5099 - the `device` special category and the per-device fallback
5: source: S318 - hyprwm/Hyprland src/config/values/ConfigValues.cpp @ efb5099 - the `input:kb_*` and `input:scroll_factor` options and defaults
6: source: S319 - hyprwm/Hyprland src/devices/IKeyboard.cpp @ efb5099 - stored rules, the fallback keymap, kb_file, overridden keymaps
7: source: S320 - hyprwm/Hyprland src/debug/HyprCtl.cpp @ efb5099 - the `devices` request and its JSON fields
8: source: S321 - hyprwm/Hyprland src/helpers/MiscFunctions.cpp @ efb5099 - how a device name is normalized
9: source: S322 - hyprwm/Hyprland src/devices/VirtualKeyboard.cpp @ efb5099 - a virtual keyboard adopts the keymap its client sends
10: source: S323 - hyprwm/Hyprland src/protocols/VirtualKeyboard.cpp @ efb5099 - the names virtual keyboards get
11: source: S324 - hyprwm/hyprlang src/config.cpp @ v0.6.8 (the host's hyprlang 0.6.8-5) - comments, continuations, keyed special categories
12: source: S325 - hyprwm/hyprland-wiki content/Configuring/Keywords.md @ ecc06f3 (2026-04-20) - the documented per-device block
13: verified-at: 2026-10-26
14: recheck: on-release - when `hyprctl version` leaves efb50993 or hyprlang leaves 0.6.8 (or 90d at the latest) re-pull the ten pins and re-run the counter; observed 2026-10-26 (w1/ScoutRecheck, not a pin): Hyprland main has no src/config/legacy/ and configures devices through Lua, so a release past 0.56.x drops the `device { }` block form this entry and tools/mado-devices.py describe

16: Lane: mado-shokunin. Round: 46.
17: 
18: ## What the evidence licenses
19: 
20: | question | where the answer lives | state |
21: |---|---|---|
22: | which value of `kb_layout`, `kb_variant`, `kb_options`, `kb_model`, `kb_rules`, `kb_file` a keyboard gets | the chain below, steps 1-4 | sourced at the host's revision |
23: | what `hyprctl devices -j` prints for a keyboard and a mouse | step 5 | sourced |
24: | why a keyboard can print `us` although the config says otherwise | step 4 | sourced; the compile failure itself is not observed here |
25: | whether this host's keyboards and mice agree with their config | the live run | observed 2026-10-26 |
26: | which keymap a virtual keyboard really uses | the dump's `active_keymap` names its active layout (step 5); the rest of the keymap is UNK | partly observable |
27: 
28: ## The chain, step by step
29: 
30: 1. Parsing. The config language reads a file line by line; a line ending in a backslash is joined with the next one, and a last line that still ends in one is dropped (continuation, hyprlang-config.cpp:44-66). A line whose first character is `#` is a comment; elsewhere a `#` ends the line unless it is doubled, and a doubled `##` leaves one literal `#` in the value (comment rule, hyprlang-config.cpp:674-702). Inside a category, a key's full name is the category stack joined with `:`.
31: 2. The `device` block is a keyed special category: Hyprland registers it with `name` as its key (hyprlang special category, Hyprland-ConfigManager.cpp:518-540 registers the device block and its first values) and 47 values in all (Hyprland-ConfigManager.cpp:541-565 ends the list at pressure_range_max). Three spellings reach the same per-device store: `device { name = X ... }`, `device[X] { ... }` and `device[X]:key = value` (the bracket name is cut out of the key, hyprlang-config.cpp:291-321; the category it names is created and keyed at hyprlang-config.cpp:322-350); the wiki documents only the first (wiki-Keywords.md:70-79). A second block with the same name merges into the first: it overwrites only the keys it sets. A `device { }` block whose first assignment is not `name` is a config error: the first value must be the key (hyprlang-config.cpp:420-440 reports "first value must be the key"). The current device is forgotten when the last category closes (reset at the close, hyprlang-config.cpp:885-899), so a flat `device:kb_layout = x` outside a block runs into the same first-value-must-be-the-key error instead of setting anything.
32: 3. Per-keyboard lookup. For every keyboard, applyConfigToKeyboard reads six keymap names per device (Hyprland-InputManager.cpp:1213-1238, applyConfigToKeyboard): for example the kb_layout lookup asks the device first and the global option second (Hyprland-InputManager.cpp:1226-1231, kb_layout and its siblings). The device value counts only when the user set it; otherwise the global fallback applies (Hyprland-ConfigManager.cpp:1115-1123, set-by-user test then global fallback). The block exists when one carries the keyboard's exact normalised name (Hyprland-ConfigManager.cpp:1196-1201, deviceConfigExists; the name is the `name` the dump prints), and the STRVAL_EMPTY sentinel reads as the empty string (Hyprland-ConfigManager.cpp:1156-1168, STRVAL_EMPTY; its spelling `[[EMPTY]]` lives in a Hyprland header outside these pins). The global defaults are `us` for the layout and empty for the rest (Hyprland-ConfigValues.cpp:271-276, default XKB names). When nothing changed since the last apply, the keyboard is skipped (Hyprland-InputManager.cpp:1270-1280, did not change). Every `input:kb_*` option re-applies input devices when changed, which is how a runtime `hyprctl keyword` reaches a keyboard.
33: 4. Compiling. The five names go to setKeymap as the keyboard's current rules (Hyprland-InputManager.cpp:1282-1295, setKeymap with the names), which stores them before any compile. A non-empty kb_file (relative to the config file) is tried first and the names only when no keymap came from the file (Hyprland-IKeyboard.cpp:87-99, kb_file read before the names); so with a readable kb_file the printed names say nothing about the keymap in use, and even names that would not compile are printed as written. If no keymap results, the stored rules are reset to layout `us` with every other name empty and an error overlay names the rejected names (Hyprland-IKeyboard.cpp:101-116, fallback to us after an error overlay), which is why a typo in `kb_layout` shows up in the dump as `us`, not as the typo; a comma list of layouts collapses to that single `us`.
34: 5. What the dump shows. `hyprctl devices -j` prints the stored rules of each keyboard - rules, model, layout, variant, options - and for each pointer a `scrollFactor` with two decimals (Hyprland-HyprCtl.cpp:765-772, scrollFactor field; Hyprland-HyprCtl.cpp:785-802, layout and the other stored names). The keyboard object also carries `active_keymap` and `active_layout_index` (the compiled keymap's active layout, named in XKB's words such as `Portuguese (Brazil)`, not the config's `br`), `capsLock` and `numLock` (locked-modifier state, not the `numlock_by_default` option) and `main` (Hyprland-HyprCtl.cpp:793-801, main and the lock fields), which marks the keyboard `switchxkblayout main` acts on, not the primary hardware keyboard: on this host only the input-method virtual keyboard has it (Hyprland-InputManager.cpp:1934-1942, disableAllKeyboards clears main for physical keyboards). The `mice` list holds every pointer, touchpads included, and its `defaultSpeed` is libinput's default, not the configured sensitivity. A pointer's factor is the device block's `scroll_factor` clamped to 0..100 only when that block explicitly sets it (Hyprland-InputManager.cpp:1376-1383, scroll_factor explicitly set), and is otherwise unset, printed as -1.00 (Hyprland-InputManager.cpp:1379-1383, nullopt branch). The global `input:scroll_factor` (range 0..2, unlike the block's 0..100 clamp) never reaches the printed field (Hyprland-ConfigValues.cpp:290-300, input:scroll_factor is a separate global option), and neither does `input:touchpad:scroll_factor`.
35: 6. Virtual keyboards. A virtual keyboard (an input method such as fcitx5) is named `hl-virtual-keyboard`, followed, while `misc:name_vk_after_proc` is on (the default), by `-` and the normalised name of its client's binary or `-unknown` (Hyprland-protocols-VirtualKeyboard.cpp:11-31, hl-virtual-keyboard naming). The config's names are applied to it once at creation; when its client sends a keymap the keyboard marks the keymap overridden (Hyprland-VirtualKeyboard.cpp:36-44, virtual keyboard override flag), and setKeymap then returns early (Hyprland-IKeyboard.cpp:60-64, keymap overridden early return), so the rules the dump prints for it are the creation-time names, not a witness of the keymap in use; its `active_keymap` does name the active layout of the client's keymap.
36: 7. Names. A device's name is lower-cased with space, newline and comma replaced by `-` (Hyprland-MiscFunctions.cpp:777-789, lower-case name normalizer), an empty result becomes `unknown-device`, and a name already taken by any input device gets a `-1`, `-2` suffix (Hyprland-InputManager.cpp:2119-2131, duplicate suffix). A block written with the product name as the kernel reports it (`Razer DeathAdder V2`) never matches: the lookup compares the stored key exactly. The wiki tells the user to copy the name from `hyprctl devices` (wiki-Keywords.md:79, hyprctl devices names) and documents that per-device options overwrite the `input` ones (wiki-Keywords.md:64-70, wiki override rule).
37:

55: ## Live host run (read-only)
56: 
57: On this host (Hyprland 0.56.2 at efb50993, one `input` block in `~/.config/hypr/input.conf`, no `device` block) the join reports nine keyboards AGREE on layout `br` with options `compose:caps`, the fcitx5 input method as VIRTUAL, and four pointers AGREE at -1.00 (receipt .ufo/waves/round-46/receipts/live-devices-host-l1.txt). The touchpad's `scroll_factor = 0.4` inside `input { touchpad { } }` is a global option and, as step 5 says, is not what the dump's field shows.
58: 
59: ## Reading a DIFFER or a FALLBACK
60: 
61: - A keyboard that prints `us` and empty names while its config names something else most likely failed to compile its keymap (step 4); Hyprland also queues an error overlay naming the rejected names.
62: - A keyboard that prints other names than the config may have been changed at runtime with `hyprctl keyword`, or the config lives in a file not passed to the join, or a `source =` line the join does not follow.
63: - A device block that matches nothing was most likely written with the kernel product name instead of the normalized name (step 7).
64: - `hyprctl switchxkblayout` changes the active layout index, not the stored names, so it never turns AGREE into DIFFER.
65: - With `kb_file` set, an AGREE only says the stored names match; the keymap in use is the file's (step 4).
66: 
67: ## Limits
68: 
69: - The XKB compile itself is not reproduced: whether a given layout/variant pair exists in xkeyboard-config is not checked; only the stored names are compared.
70: - `repeat_rate`, `repeat_delay`, `numlock_by_default`, `enabled`, `keybinds`, `sensitivity`, `accel_profile` and the libinput settings are applied per device too but the dump prints none of them (it prints lock state and libinput's default speed, which are different things); they are UNK for this join.
71: - Per-device layouts do not change the keymap binds are resolved against unless `resolve_binds_by_sym = 1` (wiki-Keywords.md:124-130); the join does not check binds.
72: - Variables, `{{ }}` expressions and hyprlang directives are not modelled (the tool reports NOT_COMPARED).
73: - The Lua config of later Hyprland releases is out of scope.
74: 
75: ## Erratum (2026-10-26, round 47)
76: 
77: **E1 - the live host run reads two config files, and the citation names the L1 draft's receipt, not the landed tool's.** Corrects the parenthetical and the citation at line 57; nothing else in this entry changes.
78: 
79: *What line 57 says:* "one `input` block in `~/.config/hypr/input.conf`, no `device` block" (hyprland-input-devices-r46.md:57), citing `.ufo/waves/round-46/receipts/live-devices-host-l1.txt`.
80: 
81: *What is true:* "one `input` block" holds only for that one file read alone. The effective chain has two input files, in this order:
82: 
83: | order | file | sha256 | input block | kb_layout |
84: |---|---|---|---|---|
85: | 1 | `~/.local/share/omarchy/default/hypr/input.conf`, sourced at `~/.config/hypr/hyprland.conf:11` | `bee5aa68cbec0c428fae7a5faf7da49a30e6f78ed96e78a063f08b66942448b8` | `input {` at :2 | `us` at :3 |
86: | 2 | `~/.config/hypr/input.conf`, sourced at `~/.config/hypr/hyprland.conf:17` | `3769c92ab7660f1795cb6d0af1bcc5a5614a299b29ae8358d1b85936bdd47bf4` | `input {` at :3 | `br` at :10 |
87: 
88: So two `input` blocks reach the compositor and the first one names `us`; the join reports `br` because it parses the given files in order into one state (`tools/mado-devices.py:418-419`, `for t in config_texts: parse_config(t, st)`) and a global is a plain dict assignment (`tools/mado-devices.py:334`, `st.globals[key[len("input:"):]] = rhs`), so each later file overwrites the earlier value. The "no `device` block" half stands in both files: each has zero `device` blocks.
89:

5: # Hyprland 0.56.2 keyboard repeat rate, repeat delay and numlock (round 55)
6: 
7: ## Scope
8: 
9: Source-level path, held at the pinned tag commit, from the `input:` config keys to the per-keyboard stored values and to the wl_keyboard repeat_info calls. Every K and H row is a frozen single-line quote. Nothing here is a claim about a running compositor, a keypress or a connected client: source-level behaviour is not promoted to a claim about the running host (QUALITY 104). Host rows H01-H06 are a declaration in a file and a read, not a delivery.
10: 
11: ## What this entry settles (source-level)
12: 
13: - Declared table entries for the three keys: repeat_rate default 25 with min 0 and max 200 (K13); repeat_delay default 600 with min 0 and max 2000 (K14); numlock_by_default default false (K15). These are declarations; how the config layer enforces the range fields is UNKNOWN (below).
14: - Per-keyboard lookup: getConfigValueSafeDevice returns the global value when the device value is absent or not set by the user and the fallback name is non-empty (K16, E12). applyConfigToKeyboard reads repeat rate, delay and numlock through getDeviceInt with that fallback (K01, K02, K03). IKeyboard::setKeymap reads numlock the same way (K08).
15: - applyConfigToKeyboard stores the rate and the delay as std::max(0, value) (K04, K05) and stores numlock unfloored (K06). The unchanged-config early return at line 1271 (K07) compares the config names with the stored fields.
16: - IKeyboard::setKeymap enters its NumLock lock block only when NUMLOCKON equals 1; the if has no else (K09).
17: - updateActiveKeyboardData sends the active keyboard's stored rate and delay to the seat protocol, and only inside its if (m_keyboard) guard (K10, E09). CWLSeatProtocol::updateRepeatInfo calls repeatInfo per keyboard resource when the seat has keyboard capability (E06, E07). CWLKeyboardResource::repeatInfo sends repeat_info only when the owner is present, the resource version is 4 or later, and the rate and delay are not both equal to the last pair the resource recorded (K11, E10, K12); the initial recorded values are not shown by the lane's pins. A second push, from the CWLKeyboardResource constructor when a client binds wl_keyboard, calls repeatInfo directly with the same stored fields and does not pass through the capability test of updateRepeatInfo (E14).
18: 
19: ## What stays UNKNOWN
20: 
21: - Whether any value reaches a client. No keypress, wl_keyboard event or client bind was observed; the host rows are not delivery receipts.
22: - How the config layer treats a value outside the declared min and max of the three keys. The pins show the table declaration (K13, K14) and no enforcement site. A wave-2 source review (R55W2RevToolSource, `.ufo/waves/round-55/w2/R55W2RevToolSource.md`) reports that the legacy `.conf` path registers values without a range and that min and max are enforced only for the Lua configuration; that is a pointer, not a lane row or pin, so it stays UNKNOWN here.
23: - Whether a negative configured value can reach the early-return comparison at all. If it can, the stored floored value and the unfloored config value differ, so the early return would not fire for it. That follows from K04, K05 and K07 and is [INFERENCE]; it is not exercised.
24: - Which keyboard is m_keyboard at runtime, and therefore which stored rate and delay the seat protocol sends.
25: - Whether any live client binds wl_keyboard at version 4 or later.
26: - WHEN the three options are re-applied (a new keyboard, a config reload, `hyprctl keyword`): this entry holds only the setup caller (E08). A wave-2 source map (R55W2ScoutCallers, `.ufo/waves/round-55/w2/R55W2ScoutCallers/callers.md`) reports exactly two callers of applyConfigToKeyboard (setupKeyboard and the setKeyboardLayout loop) and five callers of setKeyboardLayout including the legacy reload path, and that repeat_delay is the one key without the input-device refresh flag on the dynamic path; that is a pointer, not a lane row or pin, so it stays UNKNOWN here.
27: - What the NumLock lock does on the host's own XKB keymap: K09 pins only the set-up line, not the keymap.
28: - What the "set": true field in host-getoption.json means. The pin records it; no pinned source row defines it.

97: ## Joining the declaration to the live read (the round-55 tool)
98: 
99: `tools/mado-kbdopts.py` (round 55, claim C2) models the config half of this chain for config text a caller supplies — device
100: value when the block set it, else the global option, else the table default (K16, K13-K15), integers as the hyprlang parser
101: reads them — and joins the result to the JSON that `hyprctl getoption <name> -j` prints. Receipt, read-only, 2026-10-28:
102: `.ufo/waves/round-55/receipts/live-kbdopts.txt`. For `~/.config/hypr/input.conf` (byte-equal to the committed pin, `cmp`) and
103: two keyboard names from `hyprctl devices -j` it prints `repeat_rate=40 repeat_delay=250 numlock=1 from=G/G/G unsupported=0`
104: for both and `AGREE` three times against the live read (the committed H04-H06 lines).
105: 
106: How to read that output without overclaiming:
107: 
108: - The host config holds no `device` block (`grep -rnE 'device\[|device \{|device\{' ~/.config/hypr` → no match, receipt
109:   `.ufo/waves/round-55/w1/R55ScoutHost/receipts-cmp-grep.txt`), so the K16 rule (read at source level) predicts the global
110:   value for every keyboard; the two per-keyboard lines are that prediction from the file bytes, [INFERENCE] about the running
111:   compositor, not an observed per-keyboard value. The tool is only given `input.conf`: a `source =` line is skipped without a
112:   count, so a value set in a file the caller did not pass is invisible to it.
113: - `getoption` returns an option by name. [INFERENCE] It takes no device argument, so `AGREE` says the running compositor's
114:   global option equals the declared one; it cannot show a device override (the reply forms are held in
115:   `hyprctl-getoption-native-forms-r40.md`, not re-claimed here).
116: - The `numLock` field of `hyprctl devices -j` is lock state, not this option (`hyprland-input-devices-r46.md` step 5); the
117:   tool never compares them.
118: - `hyprland-input-devices-r46.md:70` lists these three options as applied per device and not printed by the `hyprctl devices`
119:   dump (which prints lock state and libinput's default speed instead); K01-K03 and K08 now show the per-device read at source
120:   level and that sentence stays as history. The global `getoption -j` read does return all three (H04-H06); neither read
121:   prints a per-device value.
122: - The tool's `unsupported` count is its honesty valve, and it must be read FIRST: a value outside the modelled token set
123:   (`$` variables, `device[...]` bracket forms, hex or signed numbers, `##`) or outside the ranges it applies is counted and
124:   ignored, so the value it then prints is the default or an earlier line, NOT what Hyprland stores, and an `AGREE` against the
125:   live read is then unreliable (R55W2AuditF: `repeat_rate = 201` prints 25 and `AGREE` against a live 25). The ranges are the
126:   lane's modelling rule: K15 declares a Bool without min or max, and a wave-2 source review reports that the legacy `.conf`
127:   path does not enforce the table's min and max at all (R55W2RevToolSource), so for such a line Hyprland stores the written
128:   value and the tool does not. Known divergences from Hyprland's own parser are listed in the tool's docstring.
129: 
130: ## Adjacent ground (reported, not absorbed)
131: 
132: - The XKB compile of `kb_layout`/`kb_options` is `hyprland-input-devices-r46.md` and `xkb-keymap-compile-r48.md`; K09 only
133:   pins that numlock is set on the keymap state after that compile.
134: - What a client does with `repeat_info`, and any input-method (fcitx5) interaction, is outside the province; a sibling sweep
135:   (`.ufo/waves/round-55/w1/R55ScoutAdjacency.md`) found no sibling lane holding `repeat_rate`, `repeat_delay`,
136:   `numlock_by_default` or `repeat_info`.
137: - The host readback 40 / 250 / 1 was already held (`hyprctl-verification-layer.md:132-134`, `config-health-report-r3.md:546-548`);
138:   H01-H06 add the source chain around it, not a new host fact.

140: ## Limits
141: 
142: - Source-level only, at the pinned tag commit efb50993780079460b0cbed1363e2166a2de1d9f. No row is a claim about the running compositor; the host rows are a declaration and a read.
143: - QUALITY 104 checks: the "only" in K09 was checked against the if block at IKeyboard.cpp:122-131 (no else branch). The "only" in K10 was checked against the single-statement if at SeatManager.cpp:176-177. "Floored at zero" in K04 and K05 is the std::max(0, x) expression on that line; the line sets no upper bound.
144: - K07 is stated as what the line shows: it names the config values and the stored fields and does not state a runtime outcome. The consequence for negative values is [INFERENCE] (see UNKNOWN).
145: - getDeviceInt's empty-value branch (E01) is shown by the source; whether the three keys ever reach it is UNKNOWN.
146: - A quote proves that the cited text is at the cited line; it does not prove that a proposition sentence is entailed by it. Entailment is the independent sentence-to-pin audit, not this entry's counter.
147: - The wl_keyboard protocol text (K17, E04, E05) is the 1.26.0 specification; it is cited for the event contract and not for Hyprland's implementation of it, which is K11 and K12.
148: - Registry anchor gaps (registry rows are append-only, CORPUS rule 2, so they are recorded here rather than widened): S318 records ConfigValues.cpp:271-279, which does not reach K14 at line 280; S052 records wl_keyboard :2573-2699, which does not reach K17 at line 2741 or E04 and E05 at lines 2732 and 2740 (the wl_keyboard interface runs 2573-2752 in the pin); S316 records applyConfigToKeyboard at 1213-1295, which does not reach E08 at line 1195. The counter checks that a cited S-id is one unique registry row with eleven cells, status `mined`, a URL ending in the pin's repository path and (for Hyprland pins) containing the pinned commit; it does not read line-range anchors, so it would not catch these.
149:

The `freeze-verify-v2.py` line must end `OK`; `freeze-verify.py` verifies the superseded v1 table and also ends `OK`. The three `hyprctl getoption` commands are read-only; compare them with H04 to H06 by eye. A mismatch is a host fact, not an entry defect.

The selected row was class `Alacritty`, address `0x55b5ee84e440`, workspace `1`, `floating: false`.

64: These exact commands and outputs are also retained in `.ufo/evidence/round-12/getprop-measure.txt:68-96`. The query makes no compositor or configuration change.
65: 
66: The read values can be compared without claiming match attribution:
67: 
68: | Plane | Observed or declared value | Bounded interpretation |
69: |---|---|---|
70: | Declared terminal effect | `opacity 0.97 0.9, match:tag terminal` (`apps/terminals.conf:4`) | A declared candidate effect; class regex includes `Alacritty` (`:2`) |
71: | Declared generic effect | `opacity 0.97 0.9, match:tag default-opacity` (`windows.conf:15`) | Same pair is also declared on another tag, so value alone is ambiguous |
72: | Per-window getter | `opacity` `0.97`; `opacity_inactive` `0.9` (`getprop-measure.txt:71-75`) | Effect-read from the window's rule applicator, per tagged handler |
73: | Global options | active and inactive `1.000000`, `set: false` (`getprop-measure.txt:85-91`) | Current global option values; not the same field as per-rule match identity |
74: | Fullscreen / override | `1` / `false` (`getprop-measure.txt:77-81`) | Additional getter fields; do not identify a rule |
75: 
76: The wiki's current Lua-era opacity explanation says opacity is a product of configured opacity values; it is not used here to calculate the host's final pixel alpha. The tagged source shows this handler returns the `alpha` member from `PWINDOW->m_ruleApplicator` for `opacity` and corresponding getters for inactive/fullscreen/override. The scoped conclusion is therefore that a **window-effect value is readable**, not that a rule match or compositor-rendered result is proven.

121: ## What the 0.56.2 handler actually reads
122: 
123: At the pinned source tag, `dispatchGetProp` rejects incomplete requests with `not enough args`, stores the window selector and property, queries the selected window, and returns `window not found` if the selector is unresolved (`src/debug/HyprCtl.cpp#L1463-L1475`).
124: 
125: The opacity helper returns `alpha.valueOrDefault().alpha` for ordinary output and the `overridden` member when the override property is requested (`#L1492-L1504`). The handler's `opacity`, `opacity_inactive`, and `opacity_fullscreen` branches obtain values from the window's rule applicator; `opacity_override` is another value getter (`#L1565-L1575`). Unsupported property names reach `return "prop not found"` (`#L1644`).
126: 
127: This inspected response path returns a selected property value or an error string. It does not include a rule name, matched condition, ordered match list, or rule-source attribution. This is a bounded source-path observation at v0.56.2, not a statement about every Hyprland version or other possible instrumentation.
128: 
129: ## Corrects
130: 
131: - `corpus/knowledge/arc-synthesis.md:300` previously states: **“window-rule effects / exec-once results | no rule-read verb in Hyprland 0.56 (r2/R3-2)”**. New bounded wording: Hyprland 0.56.2 has `hyprctl getprop <regex> <property>` for per-window effect-property reads. It is effect-read only; it does not reveal which rule matched. This entry leaves `exec-once` results untouched.
132: - `corpus/knowledge/config-health-report-r3.md:449` previously states: **“Whether any window rule currently matches an open window (no rule-read verb; U4, carried).”** New bounded wording: the reason “no rule-read verb” is incorrect; `getprop` can read a window's effect value. Whether a particular rule matched remains unknown from this command because the handler returns no match attribution and the host opacity declarations share the same values. The prior notes remain unchanged.
133: 
134: ## Limits
135: 
136: - This evidence is bounded to the recorded host snapshot, selected address, and property values shown above; it is not a census of all properties or open windows.
137: - The clients projection deliberately omitted tags and titles. It therefore does not establish the selected client's current tag state or expose any title-based matching condition.
138: - Equal `0.97` and `0.9` values occur in the terminal-tag and default-opacity declarations. `getprop` does not disambiguate those declarations or enumerate all matching rules.
139: - `getprop` reads a property through the rule applicator; the inspected getter does not attest to final rendered pixel alpha. No rendering observation was performed.
140: - `workspacerules -j` is a separate workspace-rule query; its empty result is not evidence about window-rule matches.
141: - The current wiki uses the latest-git Lua-era documentation and itself marks hyprlang deprecated since 0.55. Its rule syntax/semantics are labeled `[INF-docs]`; host behavior claims here rely on v0.56.2 source and read-only host output instead.
142: - No absent-property claim is made beyond the source handler's `prop not found` branch. The host error probes and source handler span are retained as the sweep for this command path.
143: 
144: ## Rejected
145: 
146: - **Rejected claim:** “`getprop` proves which window rule matched.” The tagged implementation reads a property from `m_ruleApplicator` and returns its value, without a rule identity or match trace (`src/debug/HyprCtl.cpp#L1463-L1645`). The observed `0.97`/`0.9` pair is declared for the terminal-tag and default-opacity paths (host rule-file anchors above). **Flip condition:** a version-pinned read surface that returns a rule name or matched-condition record for this same window.
147: - **Rejected claim:** “An `Alacritty` `0.97` read proves the terminal-specific rule, rather than any other matching opacity rule, was the source.” The value agrees with the host declarations and the command's response is unattributed. **Flip condition:** evidence from a read-only, version-pinned match-attribution interface, or a controlled isolated rule state; no such mutation was attempted.
148:

154: - Pinned implementation: `https://github.com/hyprwm/Hyprland/blob/v0.56.2/src/debug/HyprCtl.cpp#L1463-L1645`.

1: # Round 56 — registration (mado, hyaku run 11, attempt 1)
2: 
3: Companion to `claims-frozen.md` (the frozen predicate sentences, targets and kill rules). This file defines every leg, grammar
4: and control the predicates name. It, `frozen-tables.json`, `fixtures/c2/`, `ctl/`, `fetch/`, `freeze-verify.py` and
5: `receipts/` are committed in ONE freeze commit BEFORE any wave `.ready`. A predicate found unsatisfiable or wrong is an honest
6: stop or a pre-data re-registration in a NEW versioned file; this file is never edited. Counters are read as written: when the
7: text is ambiguous the most literal reading wins and the seat lists the ambiguity.
8: 
9: Lane root `/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/mado-shokunin`. Base (lane tip at freeze): the `base` field of `frozen-tables.json`.
10: All counters and harnesses: `python3 -I`, read ONLY git objects at `--rev` of `--repo` (`git cat-file`, `git show`,
11: `git ls-tree`; never the working tree), honour `--work DIR` (created when missing; HOME and TMPDIR of every git child; the
12: counter itself writes nothing), embed the sha256 of `frozen-tables.json` as a constant and check it against the blob at
13: `--rev`, print exactly one result line on stdout, exit 0 only on `TARGET_MET`/`OK`, 1 on a miss, 2 on a setup error (reason
14: on stderr, nothing on stdout). Only regular blobs (modes 100644, 100755) are accepted for entry/registry/pin/tables files; a
15: symlink blob or a path crossing a symlinked directory is "absent". A harness runs counters as child processes with a scrubbed
16: `GIT_*` environment and `TMPDIR`=`--work`, builds scratch repos as `D=$(mktemp -d -p <work>) && git -C "$D" init -q` with every
17: later git call naming `-C "$D"` (assert `git -C "$D" rev-parse --show-toplevel` equals `$D` before the first commit; identity
18: via `-c user.name=ctl -c user.email=ctl@ctl`), and never writes the lane or the fleet repo. Declared limit (carried from
19: round 55, QUALITY 104/105/106): a counter that sniffs its environment, caches runs or forges a consistent tree passes a
20: non-adversarial harness; the harness is evidence against a counter that prints green without work.

22: ## §C1 — autoreload entry (coverage, sourced)
23: 
24: Entry: `corpus/knowledge/hyprland-config-autoreload-r56.md`. Pins: `.ufo/waves/round-56/fetch/` (sha256 in
25: `frozen-tables.json` `c1.pin_sha256` and `c1.host_pin_sha256`). Commit pinned: `efb50993780079460b0cbed1363e2166a2de1d9f`
26: (Hyprland v0.56.2, the host's running build, `fetch/host-version.json`).
27: 
28: **Row grammar.** A row is a line outside fenced code blocks (``` or ~~~, 0-3 leading spaces) and outside HTML comments whose
29: first cell is an id `K01..K30` or `H01..H03`. It must be exactly
30: `| <id> | <proposition> | <src> | <pin>:<line> | ` + "`" + `<quote>` + "`" + ` |` — single spaces around every pipe, no trailing byte,
31: exactly 5 cells split on the 3-byte separator ` | `, no `|` and no backslash-escaped pipe inside any cell (no frozen quote
32: contains `|`; `freeze-verify.py` proves it). `<src>` is `S###` (K rows) or the word `host` (H rows). `<pin>:<line>` equals the
33: frozen pin file name and line byte for byte. The quote cell is a single backtick, the frozen quote, a single backtick. The
34: proposition is >=40 characters (K) / >=20 (H), has no leading or trailing whitespace, and is not byte-identical to another
35: row's proposition. A missing, duplicated or malformed row is leg `row:<id>`.
36: 
37: **Legs** (every failing leg is reported, never only the first):
38: 
39: | leg | holds when |
40: |---|---|
41: | `entry_absent` | the entry path is a regular blob at `--rev` (else all entry-dependent legs are not evaluated; rows/hosts/sources/scope/content print 0) |
42: | `tables_sha` | the blob `.ufo/waves/round-56/frozen-tables.json` at `--rev` has the embedded sha256 |
43: | `prov` | the first 30 lines hold a line starting `source:`, one `verified-at: 2026-10-28` and one starting `recheck:`, in that order; every `S###` on the `source:` line(s) is a registry row; and the `source:` set contains every S-id cited by a K row |
44: | `index` | `corpus/README.md` holds exactly one line starting `| ` + backtick + `knowledge/hyprland-config-autoreload-r56.md` + backtick |
45: | `row:<id>` | the row grammar above holds |
46: | `quote:<id>` | the frozen quote is a substring of line `<line>` of the pin blob at `--rev` (K and H) |
47: | `pin_hash` | every pin blob at `--rev` hashes to its frozen sha256 |
48: | `host:<id>` | (H) the quote the ROW carries is a substring of the host pin line (independent of `quote:<id>`) |
49: | `source_cell:<id>` | (K) the row's S-id is exactly one registry row of 11 cells in `corpus/SOURCES.md` at `--rev` (id regex `^ {0,3}\|\s*S[0-9]{3,}\s*\|`), status `mined`, tier A or B, opened `2026-10-28`, whose URL (the last `http(s)://` token of the source cell) ends with `/` + `c1.pin_suffix[pin]` and contains the `rev_rule` token for that pin, and whose `used-by` cell names the entry path; and the S-id equals the frozen `sid` of that K row |
50: | `scope:<id>` | (K) in the registry row's `anchor` cell, after an occurrence of `<basename>:` (basename = last path component of `pin_suffix[pin]`), the comma-separated list of `N` or `N-M` locators contains a range that includes the row's line |
51: | `tok:<id>` | the proposition shares at least one identifier token (regex `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]{3,}`, case-sensitive) with the row's quote |
52: | `num:<id>` | every maximal digit run of the proposition (regex `(?<![A-Za-z0-9_])[0-9]+(?![A-Za-z0-9_])`) is a digit run of the quote, the integer of the row's own line, the line of another frozen row of the same pin, or one of 0,1,2,3 |
53: | `new_rows` | the rows `S383`..`S390` each satisfy `source_cell` (`new_rows=<m>/8`) |
54: 
55: Output: `MADO_AUTORELOAD_ENTRY: rev=<rev> rows=<a>/30 hosts=<b>/3 sources=<c>/30 scope=<d>/30 content=<e>/33 new_rows=<m>/8 verdict=<TARGET_MET|TARGET_MISSED> missed=<legs, comma-separated, or none>`. `a` = K rows whose `row:` and `quote:` hold; `b` = H rows whose `row:`, `quote:` and `host:` hold; `c` = K rows whose `source_cell` holds; `d` = K rows whose `scope` holds; `e` = K and H rows whose `tok` AND `num` hold. `TARGET_MET` needs every leg, `a=30 b=3 c=30 d=30 e=33 m=8`.
56: 
57: **What the counter proves and does not.** It proves quote identity at an exact pin line, registry binding and scope, and
58: lexical grounding of each proposition in its own quote (an identifier shared, no number absent from the quote). It does not
59: prove entailment: a forged proposition that reuses a quote identifier and only quote numbers passes (declared limit; the
60: sentence-to-pin audit of wave 2 and the non-author closure carry entailment, reported not counted).
61: 
62: **Harness** (`quality/check-r56-controls.py --claim c1`): prints `MADO_R56_CONTROLS: claim=c1 good=<ok|bad> controls=<k>/10 verdict=<OK|FAIL>`. `good` = the counter prints TARGET_MET on the tree at `--rev`. A control is passed when the counter, run on the control's scratch repo (a commit of the good tree with ONE mutation), exits 1 with `missed` containing the control's required leg and printing no TARGET_MET. Controls (mutations on the committed tree; the harness holds the expectations; the counter knows nothing about them):
63: 1. `entry-absent` — delete the entry file → `entry_absent`.
64: 2. `junk-placeholders` — replace every K and H proposition by `placeholder proposition text for row <id>, not a claim about any source line` (K) / `placeholder host proposition <id>, not a read` (H) → `tok:` and `content` shortfall (`e<33`).
65: 3. `false-number` — in the row K04 proposition append ` The value is capped at 200 by this call.` → `num:K04`.
66: 4. `cross-pin-source` — K10 cites `S385` in place of `S383` → `source_cell:K10`.
67: 5. `scope-swap` — add a scratch registry row `S399` with the SAME URL as S383, status mined, tier A, opened 2026-10-28, used-by naming the entry, anchor `ConfigWatcher.cpp:1-10`, and make K10 cite `S399` → `scope:K10` (and `source_cell:K10`: the frozen sid differs).
68: 6. `provenance-dangling` — append `, S999` to the `source:` line → `prov`.
69: 7. `dropped-row` — delete row K17 → `row:K17`.
70: 8. `edited-quote` — change one character inside the K05 quote cell → `quote:K05`.
71: 9. `pin-byte` — change one byte of `fetch/Hyprland-ConfigWatcher.cpp` → `pin_hash`.
72: 10. `new-row-missing` — delete the registry row S390 → `new_rows` (and `source_cell:` of the K rows citing S390).

74: ## §C2 — `tools/mado-watchplan.py` (operability, capability acquisition)
75: 
76: A single-file read-only python3 tool: predicts the inotify watch list Hyprland 0.56.2 (hyprlang era, `configProvider`
77: hyprlang) registers for a config tree, and joins `misc:disable_autoreload` to a `hyprctl getoption` dump. It reads only the
78: files named by its arguments, the files its `source =` lines reach, and `os.stat`/`os.lstat`/`os.path.realpath`/`glob` on
79: those paths; it starts no process, opens no socket, writes nothing.
80: 
81: **Arguments** (two-token form only, any order): `--config PATH` exactly once (absolute), `--home DIR` at most once
82: (absolute), `--display-root DIR` at most once (absolute), `--getoption PATH` at most once (absolute). Anything else — a
83: missing `--config`, a repeated option, a relative path, an unknown token, an option without value — is setup reason `usage`.
84: `--selftest` alone is informational and is not part of PRED-C2-r56. The harness replaces the token `{FX}` in every `args.json`
85: argument by the absolute path of the fixture directory (`fixtures/c2/F##`, `fixtures/c2-holdout/H##`) inside its scratch
86: export, then runs the tool with cwd = the export root. `--display-root DIR`: every printed path equal to DIR or starting with
87: DIR + `/`, where DIR is either the given string (trailing `/` removed) or its `os.path.realpath`, prints with that prefix
88: replaced by `$ROOT`. Setup failure: stdout exactly `MADO_WATCH: SETUP <reason>`, exit 2, reason `usage` | `unreadable` (the
89: `--getoption` path is missing, unreadable, a directory or non-regular) | `too_large` (any file the tool reads — the config,
90: a sourced file or the getoption file — larger than 1048576 bytes) | `bad_json` (a non-empty `--getoption` line, stripped,
91: that is not a JSON object). The `--config` path is never a setup error: it is always the first entry of the path list; it is
92: parsed when it is a regular file and otherwise (missing, directory, unreadable) only listed (`type=missing` when `lexists`
93: fails, as fixture F14 shows). A sourced file that cannot be read is listed and not parsed.
94: 
95: **Model** (a deliberate subset of what Hyprland 0.56.2 does; everything outside it is counted `unsupported`, never guessed):
96: - Files decode as UTF-8 with errors replaced and split on `\n`. The category stack resets at each file's start. A raw line is
97:   stripped of space, tab, CR, LF, VT, FF; an empty line or a line whose first character is `#` is skipped.
98: - A line without `=`: exactly `}` pops the stack; a line ending in `{` pushes its text before the `{` (stripped); any other
99:   line is ignored.
100: - A line with `=`: LHS and RHS are the stripped texts before/after the FIRST `=`. LHS `source`: if the stack is non-empty
101:   `unsupported` +1 and the line ends; else the RHS cut at its first `#` and stripped is the source path (below). An LHS
102:   starting with `$` is ignored. KEY = the stack joined with `:` then `:` then LHS when the stack is non-empty, else LHS.
103:   Only KEY `misc:disable_autoreload` matters: if the line contains `$`, `{` or a backslash, or the RHS contains `##`,
104:   `unsupported` +1 and the line ends; else VALUE = RHS cut at its first `#` and stripped; VALUE parses as hyprlang
105:   `configStringToInt`: starts with `true`, `on` or `yes` → 1 (checked first); else starts with `false`, `off` or `no` → 0;
106:   else matches `[0-9]+` entirely → that decimal integer; anything else → `unsupported` +1, line ignored. The last accepted
107:   value in file-processing order wins.
108: - **Source handling** (legacy `handleSource`, `fetch/Hyprland-legacy-ConfigManager.cpp:1810-1850`; `absolutePath`,
109:   `fetch/Hyprland-MiscFunctions.cpp:61-83`): `cur` = the path of the file being parsed (the main config path at depth 0). RAW =
110:   the source path. (1) `len(RAW) < 2` → event `ERROR reason=bogus`, nothing else. (2) RAW starts with `~` and no `--home` was
111:   given, or RAW contains a backslash → `unsupported` +1, line ends. (3) VALUE = absolutePath(RAW, cur): RAW[0]=`~` → `--home` +
112:   RAW[1:]; RAW[0]=`/` → RAW; else curdir = `cur[:rfind('/')]` (all of `cur` if it holds no `/`); RAW[0]=`.`: RAW starts `../` →
113:   parent = `curdir[:rfind('/')]` (all of curdir if none) + RAW[2:]; RAW starts `./` → curdir + RAW[1:]; any other `.`-leading
114:   RAW (e.g. `.hidden`, `..x`) → curdir + `/` + RAW; else (not `.`-leading) curdir + `/` + RAW. (4) MATCHES = `sorted(glob.glob(VALUE))`
115:   (bytewise order; python `glob` semantics: `*`/`?`/`[..]`, no brace expansion, leading-dot names need a leading-dot pattern; a
116:   dangling symlink counts as a match). No match → event `ERROR reason=nomatch`. (5) For each match M in order: V =
117:   absolutePath(M, cur) (identity for absolute M); `os.stat(V)` failing (including a dangling symlink) → event `ERROR
118:   reason=inaccessible` and the REST of this line's matches are skipped; a regular file → append (V, via) to the path list
119:   (BEFORE parsing it) and parse V recursively with `cur`=V and depth+1, unless depth+1 > 16, in which case `unsupported` +1 and
120:   V is recorded but not parsed; a directory → event `SKIP reason=directory`; anything else → event `SKIP reason=nonregular`.
121:   `via` = `<display(cur)>:<line number, 1-based>`; the main config's via is `main`.
122: - A `--config` path is always the first list entry (via `main`) and is parsed when it is a regular file.
123: - **Watch list** (`fetch/Hyprland-ConfigWatcher.cpp:32-76`): if the last accepted `misc:disable_autoreload` value is non-zero the
124:   watch list is EMPTY (`autoreload=off`); otherwise every list entry in order becomes one primary watch: `type=dir` if
125:   `os.path.isdir(path)` (follows symlinks), else `type=file` if `os.path.lexists(path)`, else `type=missing`; mask text
126:   `IN_CLOSE_WRITE|IN_DONT_FOLLOW` for file and missing, `IN_CLOSE_WRITE|IN_DONT_FOLLOW|IN_CREATE|IN_DELETE|IN_MOVED_TO|IN_MOVED_FROM`
127:   for dir. If `os.path.islink(path)` and `os.path.realpath(path, strict=True)` succeeds, one further watch follows immediately:
128:   `role=canonical`, `path`=that realpath, `type=file`, mask `IN_CLOSE_WRITE`. Duplicate paths yield duplicate watches.
129:   Declared divergences from Hyprland: the depth cap of 16 (Hyprland has none), `glob` bytewise order versus the C library's
130:   locale collation, GLOB_TILDE `~user` forms, the Lua config provider, runtime `hyprctl keyword misc:disable_autoreload`.
131: 
132: **Output** (stdout; every line starts `MADO_WATCH` or `MADO_WATCH_LIVE`; exit 0 on a completed run):
133: ```
134: MADO_WATCH: config=<display(config)> autoreload=<on|off> disable_autoreload=<unset|N> paths=<list length> unsupported=<U> errors=<E>
135: <events in processing order>   MADO_WATCH: ERROR via=<via> source=<RAW> reason=<bogus|nomatch|inaccessible>
136:                                MADO_WATCH: SKIP via=<via> path=<display(V)> reason=<directory|nonregular>
137: MADO_WATCH: n=<k> role=<primary|canonical> path=<display(path)> type=<file|dir|missing> mask=<mask> via=<via>      (k counts watch lines from 1)
138: MADO_WATCH_LIVE: option=misc:disable_autoreload config=<0|1> live=<int|?> verdict=<AGREE|DIFFER|UNKNOWN>            (only with --getoption)
139: MADO_WATCH: DONE watches=<count of watch lines> unsupported=<U> errors=<E> live=<NONE|AGREE|DIFFER|UNKNOWN>
140: ```
141: `config` in the live line is 1 when the last accepted value is non-zero, else 0 (also when unset). Each non-empty
142: `--getoption` line is a JSON object; an object with a string `option` and an integer (not boolean) `int` records that option's
143: live value (a later line wins); other objects are ignored; a non-object JSON value is `bad_json`; no record → `live=?`,
144: `UNKNOWN`. `AGREE` when the live int equals `config`, else `DIFFER`.
145: 
146: **Fixtures.** `fixtures/c2/F01..F18/{args.json,expected.out,expected.rc,note.txt,tree/...}`; trees may hold git symlinks (the
147: harness exports with `git archive` + `tar -x`, which preserves them; a harness that dereferences or drops a symlink is wrong).
148: Expected output is stored byte for byte. **Hold-out:** eight fixtures H01-H08 of the same layout were generated by the L1
149: before the wave and are held outside the repository; the commitment `c2.holdout_commit_sha256` is the sha256 over, for each
150: file or symlink under the hold-out root in sorted UTF-8 byte order of the relative path, the line
151: `<relpath>\0<f|l>\0<hex sha256 of the file bytes (f) or of the link text (l)>\n`. The same commitment over
152: `fixtures/c2` equals `c2.fixture_commit_sha256`. The L1 reveals the hold-out into `fixtures/c2-holdout/` at integration; seats
153: are not told where it is held and do not look for it.
154: 
155: **Harness** (`quality/check-r56-controls.py --claim c2`; optionally `--holdout DIR`, default `fixtures/c2-holdout` in the export):
156: exports the tool, `fixtures/c2`, `fixtures/c2-holdout` and the three `ctl/` stubs at `--rev` into a scratch dir, runs each as
157: a child process (`python3 -I <tool> <args>`; cwd = export root; scrubbed env), compares stdout bytes and exit status with
158: `expected.out`/`expected.rc`, recomputes the two commitments, and prints
159: `MADO_R56_C2: rev=<rev> fixtures=<a>/18 holdout=<b>/8 holdout_commit=<ok|bad|absent> controls=<k>/3 verdict=<TARGET_MET|TARGET_MISSED>`.
160: `controls=3/3`: `ctl/const-host.py` passes at most 1 of the 26 fixtures; `ctl/empty.py` passes 0; `ctl/lookup-committed.py`
161: passes 18/18 committed and 0/8 hold-out. `holdout_commit=ok` means the commitment over the hold-out root equals
162: `c2.holdout_commit_sha256`.
163: 
164: ## §C3 — registry back-references (calibration)
165: 
166: **Definitions** (all at `--rev`). ENTRIES = regular blobs `corpus/knowledge/*.md` (direct children only). ROWS = registry rows
167: of `corpus/SOURCES.md` (id regex `^ {0,3}\|\s*S[0-9]{3,}\s*\|`, split on `|` after stripping the outer pipes, exactly 11
168: cells; a row with another cell count is a setup error). CITE(entry) = the set of tokens matching
169: `(?<![A-Za-z0-9])S[0-9]{3}(?![0-9])` on lines of the entry that are outside fenced code blocks (``` / ~~~ fences with 0-3
170: leading spaces). USEDBY(row) = every match of `corpus/knowledge/[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+\.md` in cell 11. SRC(entry) = tokens on lines
171: starting `source:` among the entry's first 15 lines.
172: - `missing` = the number of pairs (S, entry) with S a registry row id, S in CITE(entry) and the entry path not in USEDBY(S).
173: - `extra` = the number of pairs (S, path) with path in USEDBY(S) and (path not an ENTRIES member OR S not in CITE(path)).
174: - `dangling` = the number of pairs (entry, S) with S in SRC(entry) and S not a registry row id.
175: Cells other than `used-by` are never touched by the fix; `used-by` changes are logged old→new in the round lesson (CORPUS
176: rule 2). A non-registry id token such as the draft id `S243` in `request-limits-upstream-r31.md:33` is neither `missing` (it
177: is not a row id) nor `dangling` (not on a `source:` line) and stays as is.
178: 
179: Output: `MADO_USED_BY: rev=<rev> entries=<E> rows=<R> missing=<m> extra=<x> dangling=<d> verdict=<TARGET_MET|TARGET_MISSED> missed=<missing,extra,dangling|none>` — `TARGET_MET` only at `m=0 x=0 d=0`; exit 0/1/2 as above. Baseline measured at the base revision: `entries=70 rows=319 missing=65 extra=0 dangling=0`.
180: 
181: **Harness** (`quality/check-r56-controls.py --claim c3`): `MADO_R56_CONTROLS: claim=c3 good=<ok|bad> controls=<k>/6 verdict=<OK|FAIL>`. Controls (one mutation each on a scratch commit of the good tree at `--rev`; the counter must exit 1 and `missed` must contain the leg):
182: 1. `base-registry` — replace `corpus/SOURCES.md` by its bytes at the frozen base revision (`base` in the tables; the object must be readable in `--repo`) → `missing`.
183: 2. `fill-all` — set the used-by cell of EVERY row to the space-joined list of ALL entry paths → `extra`.
184: 3. `drop-one` — remove the path of the first entry (sorted) from the used-by cell of the first row (sorted by id) that lists it → `missing`.
185: 4. `dangling-source` — append ` S999` to the `source:` line of the first entry (sorted) → `dangling`.
186: 5. `ghost-path` — add `corpus/knowledge/no-such-entry.md` to the used-by cell of S001 → `extra`.
187: 6. `fenced-cite` — move one entry's only citation of some row into a fenced block (the harness picks, by sorted order, the first (S, entry) pair where entry cites S exactly once on a non-fence line; it wraps that line in a ``` fence) while used-by still lists it → `extra`.
188: 
189: ## Seat boundaries (every seat)
190: 
191: A seat writes only under `<lane>/.ufo/waves/round-56/<wave>/<SeatName>/` and its report file `<SeatName>.md` beside it; it never
192: edits tracked lane files, the fleet repo, sibling lanes, `jj` or the git index, never commits, and never touches host
193: configuration or the compositor (host reads: `hyprctl getoption`, `hyprctl version -j`, `stat`, reads of `~/.config/hypr`).
